Should we be concerned about the easing in household spending and after-tax wage growth for those with the top 5% of 
incomes? For now, probably not, as in our view this spending growth is likely being driven by wealth effects rather than wage 
gains. Absent a sharp correction in equity markets, there is little reason to expect them to retrench their spending. However, 
developments here are worth watching as this cohort comprises a significant proportion of overall consumer spending. They also 
tend to make outsized contributions to several discretionary spending categories (Exhibit 11).  
Exhibit 11: The top 5% of households by income make up a disproportionate amount of spending in highly discretionary categories

With summer gone, thoughts turn to the holidays and BNPL… 
Now that summer is over, the next big gauge of consumer momentum is how people spend during the run-up to, and over, the 
holiday season. It is early days, but September spending on holiday items ran well ahead of 2025 (Exhibit 12). Given the backdrop 
of firmer after-tax wage growth and breadth across retail and services categories, this is perhaps not surprising. However, some 
of this spending strength could be due to rising prices as opposed to more purchases.

A concern often raised as we move into the holidays is whether consumers will lean on less traditional forms of credit such as 
buy now, pay later (BNPL) to finance their spending. Frequent BNPL users tend to have higher credit card utilization rates (read 
more in: Do consumers have wiggle room to absorb higher gas prices?).  
Looking at the share of households in Bank of America deposit data with a BNPL payment, we do tend to see a seasonal rise 
over the holidays (Exhibit 13), though in past years, these increases have quickly reversed. Further, while the underlying trend in 
BNPL usage is upwards, there are no signs of a sharp acceleration. We do, however, see some evidence that BNPL is being used 
to finance more expensive purchases, with the share of households making repayments above $500 rising, albeit relatively slowly 
(Exhibit 14).  
Exhibit 13: 8.3% of Bank of America households had a BNPL 
payment in September

BANK OF AMERICA INSTITUTE

The tendency towards greater usage is occurring across income cohorts, though is somewhat more pronounced for lower-
income households (Exhibit 15). However, higher-income households have the largest share of BNPL payments above $500 
(Exhibit 16). Overall, in our view, the increase in the share of BNPL users heading into the season does not appear too 
concerning, though the rate of usage for some BNPL consumers does bear watching.

Methodology  
Selected Bank of America transaction data is used to inform the macroeconomic views expressed in this report and should be considered in the 
context of other economic indicators and publicly available information. In certain instances, the data may provide directional and/or predictive 
value. The data used is not comprehensive; it is based on aggregated and anonymized selections of Bank of America data and may reflect a 
degree of selection bias and limitations on the data available. 
Any payments data represents aggregated spend from US Retail, Preferred, Small Business and Wealth Management clients with a deposit 
account or credit card. Aggregated spend include total credit card, debit card, ACH, wires, bill pay, business/peer-to-peer, cash, and checks.  
Any Small Business payments data represents aggregate spend from Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit 
card. Payroll payments data include channels such as ACH (automated clearing house), bill pay, checks and wire. Bank of America per Small 
Business client data represents activity spending from active Small Business clients with a deposit account or a Small Business credit card and 
at least one transaction in each month. Small businesses in this report include business clients within Bank of America and generally defined as 
under $5mm in annual sales revenue.  
Unless otherwise stated, data is not adjusted for seasonality, processing days or portfolio changes, and may be subject to periodic revisions.  
The differences between the total and per household card spending growth rate (if discussed) can be explained by the following reasons:  
1. Overall total card spending growth is partially boosted by the growth in the number of active cardholders in our sample. This could be due to 
an increasing customer base or inactive customers using their cards more frequently. 
2. Per household card spending growth only looks at households that complete at least five transactions with Bank of America cards in the 
month. Per household spending growth isolates impacts from a changing sample size, which could be unrelated to underlying economic 
momentum, and potential spending volatility from less active users. 
3. Overall total card spending includes small business card spending while per household card spending does not. 
4. Differences due to using processing dates (total card spending) versus transaction date (per household card spending). 
5. Other differences including household formations due to young adults moving in and out of their parent’s houses during COVID. 
Any household consumer deposit data based on Bank of America internal data is derived by anonymizing and aggregating data from Bank of 
America consumer deposit accounts in the US and analyzing that data at a highly aggregated level. Whenever median household savings and 
checking balances are quoted, the data is based on a fixed cohort of households that had a consumer deposit account (checking and/or savings 
account) for all months from January 2019 through the most current month of data shown.  
Bank of America aggregated credit/debit card spending per household includes spending from active US households only. Only consumer card 
holders making a minimum of five transactions a month are included in the dataset. Spending from corporate cards are excluded. Data 
regarding merchants who receive payments are identified and classified by the Merchant Categorization Code (MCC) defined by financial 
services companies. The data are mapped using proprietary methods from the MCCs to the North American Industry Classification System 
(NAICS), which is also used by the Census Bureau, in order to classify spending data by subsector. Spending data may also be classified by other 
proprietary methods not using MCCs.  
Store category aggregation for “more discretionary”, “partly discretionary”, and “mostly necessity” is defined as follows: 
More discretionary: furniture, electronics, building materials, hobbies, miscellaneous stores, nonstore retail, clothing. 
Partly discretionary: auto, health and personal care, general merchandise. 
Mostly necessity: Gasoline, food and beverage.  
We consider a measure of services necessity spending that includes but is not limited to groceries, utilities, and gasoline. Discretionary services 
includes but is not limited to charitable donations, leisure travel, entertainment, and professional/consumer services. Discretionary retail 
includes but is not limited to general merchandise, miscellaneous, clothing, electronics and furniture. It excludes categories like groceries and 
gasoline. Holiday spending is defined as items in which spending in the November-December period is usually at least 20% of total annual 
spending on the category.  Value and premium grocers are determined judgmentally on a proprietary analysis of merchants by equity analysts. 
The “more discretionary” retail category includes furniture, electronics, building materials, online marketplaces, clothing retailers and 
miscellaneous stores; while “partly discretionary” retail contains auto parts and services, health and personal care retailers, general merchandise 
and “mostly necessity”  retail contains gas and groceries. 
Lower, middle and higher household income cuts in Bank of America credit and debit card spending per household, and consumer deposit 
account data are based on quantitative estimates of each households’ income. These quantitative estimates are bucketed according to terciles, 
with a third of households placed in each tercile periodically. The lowest tercile represents ‘lower income’, the middle tercile represents ‘middle 
income’ and the highest tercile ‘higher income’. The income thresholds between these terciles will move over time, reflecting any number of 
factors that impact income, including general wage inflation, changes in social security payments and individual households’ income. The 
income and tercile in which a household is categorised are periodically re-assessed.  
Generations, if discussed, are defined as follows: Gen Z, born after 1995; Younger Millennials: born between 1989-1995; Older Millennials: born 
between 1978-1988; Gen Xers: born between 1965-1977; Baby Boomer: 1946-1964; Traditionalists: pre-1946. 
Any reference to card spending per household on gasoline includes all purchases at gasoline stations and might include purchases of non-gas 
items. 
Additional information about the methodology used to aggregate the data is available upon request.

## Document 298: Fed builds credibility with hike; one more likely in Dec

Source PDF: all report pdf/Fed builds credibility with hike; one more likely in Dec.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Fed builds credibility with 
hike; one more likely in Dec 
Summary 
Event: The US Federal Reserve hiked its key interest rate by 
25bps to 3.75-4.0%, in a unanimous decision. This marks the 
first hike since 2023, reversing the easing cycle that had 
taken rates down from 4.25% in September 2025 to a trough 
of 3.75% by December 2025. The revised projections 
indicate the Fed expects to raise rates by an additional 25bps 
this year, hold rates in 2027, and cut 25bps in both 2028 and 
2029. It upgraded the long-run rate slightly to 3.2%.  
Market reaction: The US yield curve mildly flattened , while 
the S&P500 index fell 0.5%. The policy rate sensitive US 2 -
year government bond yield jumped 7 bps to 4. 74% as 
markets factored in  three more 25bps hikes by June 2027 . 
The US 10-year yield rose 2bps to 5.02%, its highest since 
2007, while the 30-year yield was little changed. The USD 
index (DXY) rose 0.6%, breaking past its 50- and 100-DMAs. 
Investment strategy: Today’s move realises what we had 
been flagging in recent weeks i.e. the rising risk of a Fed rate 
hike following Fed Chair Warsh’s Jackson Hole speech and 
recent economic data prints, namely: a pick-up in job creation 
and inflation over the summer and a sharp rebound in oil 
prices due to the re-escalation of the Middle East conflict.  
This hike helps the Fed rebuild credibility as an inflation 
fighter. Given the unanimous decision to hike rates this week, 
we believe there will likely be one more hike before the end 
of this year, and one more in H1 2027 as we expect robust 
growth amid strong AI investments and a tight labour market, 
keeping inflation elevated.  That scenario would still be less 
than the three more rate hikes money markets are pricing 
after the latest Fed move. We believe policy rates can return 
to the 4.0 –4.25% range by end-2027, with the possibility of 
cuts in H2 2027  as the impact of tariffs and high oil prices 
start to fade towards the second half of 2027.   
Today’s refreshed Fed policy rate path is mildly bullish for the 
USD index; so we revise up our 3 -month target to 100. We 
also revise up our 3-month 10-year yield target to 5.0-5.25%.  
 The USD rose, while stocks fell after the Fed hiked rates 
and signalled one more rate hike this year; ultra-long 
bonds rose slightly as the Fed rebuild its inflation-
fighting credentials 
Asset performance: 15-Sep close to 16-Sep close

2026-27 and inflation projection for 2026; however, it 
still sees inflation cooling over the next two years

Backdrop to the rate hike 
In hiking rates, Fed Chair Warsh reaffirmed the inflation 
threat to the US economy. US headline inflation accelerated 
to 0.4% m/m in August and stayed elevated on an annualised 
basis at 3.4% y/y. Energy prices , driven by the Middle East 
conflict, were a primary contributor. Oil prices have extended 
gains since the August inflation report, surging past USD 
100/bbl. Meanwhile, core inflation rose 0.3% m/m in August, 
beating estimates for a 0.2% rise . Also, US producer prices 
jumped 0.4% m/m in August, the most since May, and 5.4%  
y/y, further reinforcing the inflationary backdrop. 
The inflation report follow ed a stronger -than-expected jobs 
report for August. US monthly job creation has surged to an 
average 80,000 this year, from 10,000, tightening a job 
market that already faces a supply crunch from the Trump 
administration’s clampdown against immigration.  The 
combination of an acceleration in job creation and inflation 
over the summer, combined with a rebound in oil prices, 
supported our expectation for a Fed rate hike this week.  
Removing “a dose of accommodation” 
Warsh framed the decision to hike as removing "a dose of 
accommodation", rather than the start of an aggressive rate 
hiking cycle, and we agree with that characterisation. The aim 
is to bring financial and credit conditions more in line with the 
Fed's price stability objectives. To quote Warsh: “Our 
predominant focus is on the price stability side of our 
mandate. The plain fact is that inflation is too high, and has 
been for too long….This  summer’s inflation readings do not 
tell me that underlying trends have meaningfully improved.” 
He added that many categories in the last consumer and 
producer inflation reports are still showing price increases 
above 3% on a six- and 12-month basis.  
The unanimous vote to hike rates, contrasts with a 9 -3 split 
vote in July to hold rates. When combined with the pencilling 
in of one more hike by December, this suggests a majority of 
Fed policymakers have turned more hawkish over the 
summer. In doing so, they have helped rebuild the Fed’s 
inflation-fighting credentials and its independence against 
President Trump’s pressures to cut rates. This explains why 
long-end bond yields were stable after this week’s hike. 
Nevertheless, Warsh provided little guidance on the future 
rate path, as he held what was reportedly the shortest post -
meeting press conference on record for a Fed chair . This is 
consistent with his stated preference for less central bank 
communication. We expect more clarity once the Fed task 
forces submit their reports by year end. 
— Jonathan Liang, CIO for Fixed Income and FX   
Rajat Bhattacharya, Senior Investment Strategist

## Document 300: Financial conditions in the Eurozone_ a tightening temporarily tempered by credit volumes (2026-10-07)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Financial conditions in the Eurozone_ a tightening temporarily tempered by credit volumes (2026-10-07).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

7 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
FINANCIAL CONDITIONS IN THE EUROZONE: A TIGHTENING TEMPORARILY TEMPERED BY CREDIT VOLUMES
Thomas Humblot
*LATEST OBSERVATION: 5 OCTOBER 2026  **ESTIMATE
Since June 2026, the ECB has been publishing 1 a new indicator that helps to assess the transmission of its monetary policy to the economy:
the Broad Intermediation Gauge 2 (ECB-BIG). This new tool has revealed that, thus far, strong credit growth has limited the restrictive impact

of tightening financial conditions caused by rising interest rates in the Eurozone. However, this offsetting effect is beginning to fade.
The ECB-BIG: a new indicator for a broader view of financial conditions
The ECB-BIG complements the range of indicators used by the European Central Bank (ECB) to assess the effective transmission of its monetary
policy. This new tool provides a comprehensive view of how restrictive (or expansive) financial conditions are in the Eurozone. It stands out

for its approach that focuses more specifically on intermediation conditions. Therefore, the ECB-BIG provides a more comprehensive view than
most other financial condition indicators of financial conditions. In fact, it covers variables relating to the volume of bank lending to households
and businesses (the ‘volume’ component). Traditionally, this type of indicator tends to focus more on market variables (primarily interest and
exchange rates), share prices and corporate spreads. In addition, the ‘price’ component of the ECB-BIG is extended to include the cost of bank

loans, which is another new feature.
The rise in interest rates was temporarily offset by increased lending
By aggregating the variables relating to banking and non-banking intermediation conditions, the ECB-BIG shows that rising interest rates
(negative price effect) were offset in particular by lending volumes, which have continued to rise in 2026 (positive volume effect). A more
‘traditional’ indicator of financial conditions, the Macro-Finance FCI (MF-FCI) — which is, incidentally, included in the ECB-BIG — reveals a

much more pronounced tightening over the same period (see the orange curve in the chart). However, this offsetting of the price effect by the
volume effect is now beginning to wane, a sign that monetary policy is being effectively transmitted to the economy. Financial intermediaries
are gradually passing on the rise in rates to their customers (see the dotted green lines, which represent the composite indicators of the cost
of new loans to households and businesses, combining interest rates for different maturities), while lending volumes are falling. As a result,

between July and August 2026, seasonally adjusted flows of loans to households and businesses almost halved.
1 P . Lane, 2026, The Outlook for the Euro Area Economy, Background slides for Reuters NEXT Europe, London, 16 June 2026
2 A. Allayioti et al., 2026, The ECB-BIG index: tracking credit conditions in the euro area, ECB Economic Bulletin, Issue 5/2026
SOURCE: ALLAYIOTI AND AL. (2026), ECB, BLETZINGER ET AL. (2026), BNP PARIBAS ESTIMATES
Credit volumes have temporarily cushioned the rise in interest rates

In the longer term, financial conditions are expected to tighten, including as measured by the ECB-BIG
Despite the very sharp widening of sovereign spreads between 28 September and 2 October, financial conditions as measured by the
MF-FCI did not tighten, with sovereign yields actually falling over this period (with the exception of the OAT and the BTP), for example.

Nevertheless, the high volatility of the bond market, particularly in the sovereign segment, is likely to continue to affect financial
conditions. Looking slightly further ahead, the ECB’s key interest rate rises — the one that occurred on 16 September and another that we
anticipate for December 2026 (both +25 bp) — are likely to result in a more pronounced tightening. With a time lag of a number of weeks,
financial conditions, as defined by the ECB-BIG, are also expected to tighten. The price effect is likely to lead to a negative volume effect

on this occasion.

► 美股 走 高，主 要 受 惠科 技 與 半導 體 表 現突
出，費城半導體指數漲勢尤其明顯 。雖然近
一週油價回落 ，稍微舒緩市場對能源通膨的
擔憂，但聯準會如預期升息1碼，後續表現動
能可能受壓抑 。此外，最新利率預測顯示 ，
官員們認為年底前至少還有一次升息 ，主因
通膨偏高 、經濟與勞動市場保持韌性 ，因此

升息可能仍有必要。
► 日股表現受惠大型科技 、半導體設備及 AI概
念股，且日本央行雖升息但並無更鷹派的態
度，日圓走弱並激勵出口類股走勢 。歐股則
缺乏明確方向 ，雖科技股彈升及油價回落提
供支撐 ，然而全球央行緊縮及公債殖利率走
升壓抑估值 ，且法國財政疑慮亦推升風險溢
價，德國因執政聯盟主要政黨在地方選舉遭
遇挫敗，增加了政策與政治上的不確定性。

訊，工業類股亦有不錯表現 ，其餘類股則漲
跌互見。

► 隨人工智慧運算需求持續攀升 ，谷歌、亞馬

優化成 本 。根據 凱 基投顧 引述產 業數 據預
估，2027年客製化晶片出貨量將年增 92%至
1,446萬顆 ，首度超 越繪圖處理器 (GPU)的
1,129萬顆 ，顯示AI晶片市場正由 GPU主導
逐步轉向多元化發展。
► 在客製化晶片方面 ，谷歌持續擴大自研張量
處理器 (TPU)的應用與對外供應 ，亞馬遜亦
積極拓展自研AI晶片(Trainium)的雲端運算服
務，預估兩者合計將佔 2027年客製化晶片出
貨量約80%。
► 隨客製 化晶 片需 求 快速增 加 ，相 關晶 片設
計、先 進製 程及 先 進封裝 需求可 望同 步提
升，涉及上述業務發展的台股供應鏈 (註)有機
會受到市場關注。

### Page 6

6
投資焦點
► 2024年全球工廠運作的工業機器人約 470萬
台，為10年前的 3倍，主要為韓國 、中國和
日本等製造業大國將其運用於汽車 、電子和
金屬加工等產業 ，並因而維持成本和品質優
勢，為競爭力重要來源之一 。雖然美國工業
機器人的普及程度仍低於亞洲主要製造業國
家，但近年積極尋求重建工業產能 、將關鍵
供應鏈遷回美國國內以及擴大半導體和製藥
生產，有可能讓美國逐步調整工業和製造層
面的策略方向。
► 傳統工業機器人與人形機器人在外觀設計與
應用場景上有明顯差異 。工業機器人通常部
署於高度標準化的環境 ，以執行範圍相對有
限且重複性高的特定作業為主 ，而人形機器
人則被設計用於多變的環境 ，並經常需要與
人類協同工作 ，因此需結合移動能力 、感知
能力與靈巧操作能力 ，才能夠執行多樣化任
務，而非受限單一功能。
工業機器人技術相對成熟且部署速度較快全球運作中的工業機器人數量穩定成長
工業機器人技術成熟且穩定成長，為製造業強國的重要競爭力來源
投資焦點

### Page 7

7
投資焦點人形機器人
► 人 形機 器 人 主 要由 結 合半 導 體 與AI 模 型的
「大腦」，並由感測器 、執行器 、線材、連
接器及輕量化電池驅動的機身所組成 ，可透
過感知環境資訊 、思考並進行協調的實體動
作。投行預估至2030年，執行器將佔整體人
形機器人成本超過 50%，為價值含量最高的
核心零組件 ，麥肯錫公司甚至認為執行器與
感測器可能成為未來供應鏈中的關鍵瓶頸。
► 機器人在實體世界中執行任務時 ，任何延遲
或連線中斷都可能帶來重大風險 ，因此相較
純數位的 AI應用 ，對即時運算 (邊緣運算 )能
力的要求更高 。而近幾年邊緣運算軟硬體能
力大幅提升 ，使本地端設備得以部署數十億
參數的 AI模型 ，並將反應延遲縮短至約 0.3
秒，使機器人能夠即時感知 、決策與行動 。
此外，過去6年風險資本對機器人、感測器及
IT 硬體的每年平均投資約 100 億美元 ， 但
2026年僅上半年即已達 280億美元 ，暗示市
場對機器人產業潛力的期待有別於過往。
機器人技術進展快速並開始吸引風險資本投資人形機器人運作有賴AI模型與大量機身零組件整合

### Page 8

8
股市
◆ 美、歐、日央行升息抑通膨 ，公債殖利率走高壓抑股債市場近期動能 。然而包括油價自高檔回落使
通膨壓力稍緩 ，以及美股第三季獲利年增率仍為雙位數成長 ，股市表現有支撐 。市場持續觀察AI資
本支出是否帶來獲利成長 ，短期內評價面壓力將增添市場動盪 ，可分散配置於不同國家與產業 ，降
低市場波動影響 。總結上述，地緣衝突、殖利率偏高及評價面壓力等紛擾帶來負面影響 ，可布局防
禦股抗震，長期而言，科技及AI半導體發展趨勢不變且基本面佳，可逢拉回時分批布局。
◆ 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點 ，加上全球利率走揚有利銀行股 。此外，地緣衝突頻傳 ，歐洲國防軍
工仍有投資題材。
策略風格：短期可增加防禦股配置，中長
期看好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分
可關注金融產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、歐洲國防
債市
◆ 全球公債殖利率攀升 ，且最新聯準會利率點陣圖顯示 ，官員預估今年底前仍有一碼升息空間 ，立場
偏向維持較高利率水平抑制通膨發展 ，加上9月製造業及服務業PMI優於預期，殖利率偏高環境建議

以中短天期債券投資為主 ，可逢利率走升時布局進行鎖利領息 。高信評債體質較佳 ，具穩定現金流
與還款能力，聚焦風險調整後利差具吸引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
◆ 可以非美貨幣債券如歐元或澳幣計價投資級債券，分散單一貨幣風險。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、科技、通訊及公用事業等
衛星配置：非美貨幣債券分散風險
匯市
◆ 聯準會升息且態度偏鷹，美元高檔盤整。
◆ 日本銀行9月升息，但前瞻指引偏中性，未如市場預期的鷹派，日圓震盪。
美元高檔整理，日圓震盪
商品
◆ 中東地緣衝突加劇推升油價，短期紛擾難解，偏高的油價可能對通膨及實體經濟帶來影響。
◆ 短期受美國雙率走高有壓 ，惟美日財政赤字壓力與去美元化使全球央行買盤依舊 ，提供黃金中長期
穩定支撐。
短期黃金有壓，中長期維持偏多看法
類別 市場看法 偏好資產
策略思維
策略思維 短期添防禦，AI與科技逢拉回布局，透過多元配置降低短期影響

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險 ，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書 ，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估 。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書 (或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用 (境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間 ，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費 ，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費 ，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金 (不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外 ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限 ，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施 )；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

### Page 2

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The six-month annualised growth rate in the Westpac–
Melbourne Institute Leading Index, which indicates the
likely pace of economic activity relative to trend three to
nine months into the future, held at –0.03% in September,
unchanged from August.

The Leading Index continues to show moderately improved
momentum compared to the middle of the year, with the
latest reads pointing to growth holding at or slightly below
trend heading into late 2026/early 2027. Growth appears
to be stabilising at a slow pace rather than stalling. That
said, both the latest fuel prices and the RBA’s September
interest rate increase are still working their way through to
the economy with impacts likely to take some of the gloss off

growth near term. Overall, Westpac expects annual growth
to come in at 1.5% for calendar 2026, a below-trend result.
The Leading Index growth rate has firmed slightly over the
last six months, rising from –0.16% back in March to –0.03%
in September. The component detail shows mixed moves over
the period. Four components have contributed more positively
with reduced drags from the Westpac-Melbourne Institute
Unemployment Expectations Index (adding +0.23ppts to

the Index growth rate); the Westpac-Melbourne Institute
Consumer Expectations Index (adding another +0.15ppts);
and the S&P/ASX200 (+0.15ppts) combining with a modest
lift in growth in US industrial production (adding +0.12ppts).
These positives have been partially offset by modest drags
from four previously stable components: dwelling approvals
(–0.34ppts); monthly hours worked (–0.08ppts); commodity
prices, measured in AUD terms (–0.07ppts) and the yield

spread (–0.05ppts).
Overall, the component mix speaks to a lack of direction.
Indeed, despite some large shocks over the last year, the
Leading Index growth rate has been relatively contained since
early 2025, holding in a narrow –0.3% to +0.5% range. This
compares to history which, outside of the COVID and GFC
shocks, has seen growth rates move between a much wider
–1.9% to +1.6% range. Of course, the apparent stability,
which is evident across all components of the index, may not

last but for now it suggests the economy has been stuck in a
bit of a holding pattern over the last two years.
Leading Index still tracking a touch below trend
The Westpac-Melbourne Institute Leading Index of
Economic Activity is designed to assist in identifying
turning points in the economy. The index combines
variables that reflect different aspects of the economy
into a single index that generally produces a more reliable
cyclical indicator than any single component taken

individually. The Leading Index of Economic Activity
provides advance information on the state of the economy
and gives early warnings of cyclical turning points.
Matthew Hassan
Head of Australian Macro-Forecasting
**Please note that this will be the last Westpac press release for
the monthly Westpac-Melbourne Institute Leading Index. We will
continue to publish regular bulletin updates through our westpaciq.
com.au website.

## Document 363: STABLECOINS_ What next for crypto assets_ (2026-10-20)

Source PDF: all report pdf/STABLECOINS_ What next for crypto assets_ (2026-10-20).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 14
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Deutsche Bank
Research

### Page 2

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?
Institutional adoption of stablecoins continues to incrementally progress in 2026. While
the frenzy from GENIUS has diminished, stablecoins’ links to the financial system have
continued to deepen, and more policymakers are seriously considering their use cases.
The market cap has hovered around ~$300bn over the past 12 months, a sign that the

stablecoin market is maturing. Stablecoin payments, while still marginal relative to
stablecoins' crypto-native purpose, are also growing, rising from around $103bn in
adjusted transaction volume in 2022 to $375bn in 2026 YTD.
For now, institutional adoption remains concentrated in cross-border payments, treasury
management, 24/7 liquidity provision, collateral, and real-time settlement.
The next big question is whether stablecoins will evolve to become merely a payments

technology, or a genuine disruptor to the global banking system. As we previously
established, the US promotion of stablecoins remains a geopolitical tool to ensure USD
dominance. Bloomberg also reported that the Trump administration is considering joint
ventures with private actors to promote USD stablecoins overseas, in a bid to boost the
dollar’s dominance and source demand for US Treasuries. Last week, Circle announced
plans to expand into 40 new markets in Southeast Asia, LatAm and the Middle East. 99%

of stablecoins (by market cap) remain pegged to USD and are largely backed by US
Treasuries.
Looking ahead, the key debate to watch is whether US regulations will allow for yield-
bearing stablecoins. Research shows that consumers say policymakers should prohibit
crypto companies from offering interest-like rewards for holding stablecoins, which will
threaten to siphon off the bank deposits that drive local lending. The US CLARITY Act
failed in Sep after lawmakers failed to agree on whether the broader crypto market

structure would support such yield-bearing stablecoins.
In this chartbook, we revisit the stablecoin market a year after the GENIUS Act was
signed. We look at: (i) how adoption has evolved, (ii) the emergence of stablecoins as
financial market plumbing via liquidity provisioning and collateral management, and (iii)
the key regulatory debates that will determine the industry’s next direction.
Payment volume has a +66% CAGR

Categories:
❑ Infrastructure: Bridges/bots/MEV that move
stablecoins between systems.

❑ Store of value: Wallets that accumulate/hold
stablecoins.
❑ Payments: Real-world payment activity
❑ Investment Trade: Trading and short-term

### Page 3

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
REFRESHER: What are stablecoins?
– Definition: Digital tokens pegged to “stable” assets (e.g. USD or gold), issued
and transferred on a blockchain, which records ownership and transactions.
– Fiat-backed stablecoins are considered fully “collateralized”: USD
stablecoin with 1mn coins in circulation would be backed by $1mn in
cash and equivalents held in reserve by the stablecoin issuer.

– Types: Fiat-, commodity-, crypto- and algorithmic-backed.
– ~99% of stablecoins market valuation is pegged to the USD.
– Market cap: $5bn (2020) to ~$310bn (2026)
– Concentrated in USDT (~60%) and USDC (~23%).
– Benefits: 24/7 transfers & settlement, lower financial intermediaries,
programmability (e.g. escrow, conditional release, automated reconciliation),
and dollar-based store of value.
– Use cases:*
– Crypto trading (~77%) remains dominant.

– Payments (5-12%) growing, cross-border transfers and remittances.
– Store of value (17-33%) especially in emerging markets.
– Other: (~2%-8%): on/off ramps and tokenized RWA settlement.
– Alternatives: CBDCs, tokenized deposits, traditional financial architecture
*Estimates on market share taken from BCG analysis.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Haver Analytics, Bloomberg Finance LP. Shows stablecoin total
market cap. Updated October 192026.
Stablecoins’ reached a plateau of ~$310bn in October 2026

### Page 4

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
ADOPTION OVERVIEW: Most stablecoin volume is still not driven by real economic
activity
Stablecoin transaction volume:
– ~$100trn (unadjusted), ~$2.42trn (adjusted) in 2026 YTD, according to
Allium.
– Chainalysis places more bullish estimates of $28trn in 2025; projected to
reach $719trn by 2035 on organic growth alone.
Stablecoin payments:
– Stablecoin payment volume grew from $151bn in 2022 to $375bn 2026 YTD

(according to VisaOnChain Analytics).
– Businesses account for the majority (58%-64%) of payments, led by B2B
payment volumes.
– Allium Sep report showed largest business payment categories include
service fees ($56bn), payroll ($43bn) and service payments ($32bn).
– Case study: Payments volume across Visa’s >160 stablecoin-linked card
programs across consumer, business and commercial activity grew ~200%
y/y.
Strongest adoption in remittances, B2B payments and dollar access in countries

with unstable currencies or weak banking systems.
– Benefits for cross-border use cases: Instant settlement, <$0.10 per
transaction, borderless, transparent, 24/7, no intermediaries. Remittance fees
are lower (0.5-3.0%) than the global fee average (~6.6%).
– Stablecoins represented 0.31% of fiat cross-border payment value in 2025.
Marginal portion but registered +64% growth increase vs fiat (+9%) according
to Allium. B2B payments had the highest cross-border share (43%).

– A 2025 IMF study also shows transaction flows reached  ~7.7% of GDP in
Latin America and ~6.7% in Africa and the Middle East.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Allium. Shows top 8 countries by received payments from Jan to August 2026.
Only the receiving end is attributed, so a $100 payment counts once, not twice. That attribution covers 13.5% of
stablecoin payments.
Stablecoins payment use case is steadily growing

CONSUMER/RETAIL PAYMENT: Still on the periphery
Consumer payments: Not yet there
– Retail adoption is still the most distant use case, especially amongst developed-market consumer credit card spend. A recent ABA survey found that only
24% of consumers think that stablecoins could provide meaningful benefits.
– Stablecoins still look more like a debit instrument than a credit product. They do not naturally replicate the consumer valueproposition of credit cards (i.e.

rewards, revolving credit, dispute rights, chargebacks, fraud protection and convenience), so there is limited incentive for developed-market consumers to
use them.
– Limited scope for payments also evident in wallets (see chart below). Very little of the stablecoin supply is being held as balances in consumer wallets.
Still, the number of retail payments is incrementally growing, with cross-border, emerging market use cases strongest
– Retail transaction volume: $2.6bn (2020) to $71bn (2024); $66bn YTD in 2026.*

– Strongest use in: (i) economies with hyperinflation; (ii) poor banking access; or (iii) high remittance fees.
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Allium data.  Shows circulating supply by holder type (Sep 2026). Categories calculated as a percentage of total circulating supply. Note that wallets account for money being held vs
payments (money moving). Business wallets in this case refer to those holding smaller balances with quick turnover (i.e. payment services, merchants, gaming), while institutional wallets hold larger balances and turn them

over quickly, i.e. financial-services firms, treasuries, and wallets. Note: *Retail sized transactions are defined as transactions of $250 or less, according to Visa OnChain Analytics.
The amount of stablecoin supply in consumer wallets stands at ~14%

### Page 6

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
CASH/LIQUDITY MANAGEMENT: Stablecoins are evolving as a major tokenized
contender
Sources: Deutsche Bank Research, taken from DB’s Tokenised money and the future of FXand Stablecoins and
agentic commerce: new rails, old frictions.
Stablecoins can help treasury management and settlement
– Stablecoins have a strong use case for settling transactions for FX,

treasury operations and collateral management i.e. intra-day repos.
– This is because stablecoins’ 24/7 settlement can address
operational frictions in cross-border transactions.
Poses significant risk to cross-border B2B transactions for banks
– Compared to traditional banks, stablecoins offer substantial relief
from transaction costs. Our equity analysts estimate total customer
processing fee savings to increase from 0 to ~$70bn in 2035, if
consumers switch from traditional banks to stablecoins.

Stablecoins is not a replacement solution to FX settlement risk
– FX settlement requires two currencies to be exchanged
simultaneously. Currently, stablecoins only serve as an intermediary
between the two currency legs, resulting in a “stablecoin sandwich”.
– Overwhelming concentration of USD-stablecoins also limits
stablecoin ability to support widespread payment-versus payment
settlement across currency pairs.
– Stablecoins also face fragmented interoperability/liquidity issues

and regulatory uncertainty across issuers/networks/jurisdictions.
– For more, read our FX report here.
Should we explain how settlement work as it is very
interesting and Rohini has a nice chart on this ?
Probably more relevant than having the stablecoin
market cap here I would say.
A more integrated post-trade process

### Page 7

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
INSTITUTIONAL ADOPTION: Corporates, card networks, and banks are integrating
stablecoins
Institutions are racing to capture stablecoin market share
– Card networks: Mastercard expanded its settlement capabilities to include
stablecoins, using USDC and Paxos-issued stablecoins.
– Institutions: Exploring stablecoins via developing in-house permissioned

blockchain experiments and/or exploring issuance of proprietary stablecoins.
– >140 companies and financial institutions helped launch Open USD
(OUSD) in Sep 2026. OUSD reached $799mn of supply in 2 weeks.
Signals shift toward consortium-backed/network-led digital dollar
models.
Are stablecoins posing a threat to traditional payment systems?
– Bullish: Stablecoins to surpass traditional rails by the 2030s (Chainalysis).
– ~17% of stablecoin-linked card volume is occurring across Visa’s

business and commercial card programs. Shows growing adoption of
stablecoins for settlement, treasury management and cross-border
commerce.
– Key concerns for banks: Fee pressure from stablecoin adoption →
bank outflows → higher reliance on wholesale funding → pressure on
net interest margins.
– Base case: However, Deutsche Bank equity analysts see more limited near-
term risk. Visa and Mastercard to remain dominant especially in advanced
markets.

– KYC, AML, sanctions screening, wallet monitoring, fraud controls and
jurisdiction-specific limits remain essential for banks.
Stablecoin transactions could match Visa and Mastercard transactions
sometime between 2031 and 2039 (Chainalysis).
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Chainalysis.
Shows projected years until crypto merchant transactions reach Visa scale.

### Page 9

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
GEOPOLITICS: Why stablecoins are a "Genius" idea for the US
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, US Treasury, Tether July 2026 report. Shows Major foreign holders of Treasury Securities.
Treasury data from June 2026.
Can sustain USD dominance
– 99% of stablecoins (by market cap) are USD-pegged.
– Stablecoins function primarily as a digital version of dollars

moving over blockchain rails, extending the dollar’s reach into
new digital environments.
– Reinforces dollar savings: The more firms invoice and transact in USD,
the more they save in USD assets. Stablecoins help preserve this
feedback loop by keeping businesses and individuals in dollar
payment rails.
Can support US debt, given share held by foreigners has fallen from
>50% in early 2000s to ~30% in early 2026.
– Stablecoin issuers total holdings: ~$170bn according to Allium x

Citizen.
– Tether: ~$141bn in direct and indirect exposure to US T-bills.
– Impact: BIS shows how $3.5bn inflow into dollar-backed stablecoins
lowers 3m T-bill yields by 2.5-3.5bp under normal conditions. Effect
more than doubles in periods of T-bill shortages.
– Forecasts: T-bills holding by stablecoin issuers could reach ~$1trn by
2028 (Treasury forecasts).
– Recent Fed SF research places it closer to $400bn by the end
of 2030, lower than the US government’s financing needs.

Stablecoin issuers’  demand for US debt could counterweight declining demand from China
Tether competes with Saudi Arabia and South Korea as major holders of US Treasuries
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Federal Reserve Bank of San Francisco, US Treasury Data. Shows Treasury
holdings, stablecoin issuers vs China.

### Page 10

Marion Laboure | Camilla Siazon |  STABLECOINS: What next for crypto assets?  |  October  20, 2026
US GENIUS ACT: Debate on rules around payment stablecoins one year on
GENIUS provisions
– Strict issuer requirements: Issuers can be private, non-bank entity, but must
meet strict federal or state requirements.
– Reserves: Requires 1:1 backing with US cash, 93 days T-bills, repos/reverse
repos, or money market funds holding those assets.
– AML/KYC/BSA compliance obligations to stablecoin issuers, aligning them

exams, and strong internal controls.
Next steps
– GENIUS takes effect no later than 18 Jan 2027, or 120 days after final
implementing regulations are issued.
– Ongoing pushback from US banks as to whether GENIUS ban on yield-
bearing stablecoins should extend to affiliate or third-party arrangements,
given many users do not hold stablecoins directly with the issuer.
– CLARITY ACT failed in Sep because of disagreements on how
exchanges/broader crypto market structure should handle stablecoins.

– The US Treasury has proposed implementing rules for payment stablecoins.
There are two ways stablecoin yield can be paid; ongoing debate over the three-
party model
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, Congressional Research Service.

RISKS: Still hotly discussed amongst policymakers and financial institutions
Bank liquidity risk
Loss of stable funding source for banks from deposit outflow (not offset by
partial flowback from stablecoin issuers’ reserve deposit)
System stability risk
Stablecoins can fail to maintain their $1 value during times of stress. Evidence shows
that USDC and Tether actually trade within 1% of their dollar value but prices can

diverge sharply during stress episodes.
Run on stablecoin issuer(s) in market stress / trust scenario triggering fire sales of
reserve assets and/or deposit withdrawals.

stablecoins, e.g. gold with only

REGULATORY COMPARISON
Overview of stablecoin regulation in the US, UK and EU UKEU US
Regime Maturity
Key Timelines
Regulatory
Framework
US
The U.S. Genius Act
EU
Markets in Crypto-Asset Regulation
(MiCAR)
Backing Assets*
• 100% backing assets in USD cash or short-term
Treasuries (≤93-day T-bills).
• Non-significant EMTs –At least 30% reserves in
same-currency bank deposits, remainder in HQLA

• Significant EMTs - At least 60% of the reserves in
bank deposits; remainder in HQLA
• Systemic stablecoins – 30% unremunerated BoE
deposits vs 70% in UK sovereign debt (HQLA)
• Non-systemic – Full 1:1 backing in HQLR assets,
minimum 5% in on demand deposits.
UK
Multi-issuance
Stance
• Rules are still being fine-tuned; no specific focus
yet on multi-issuance.
• Multi-issuance under active review; EC considers multi-
issuance permissible, an equivalence-based framework for

EU/third-country arrangements is under legislative
consideration.
• BoE considers multi-issuance unsuitable for systemic
UK use. Preference for single-issuance models backed
by single reserve (incl. Non-GPB denom. stablecoins) .
UK Cryptoasset Regime (FCA)
UK Systemic Stablecoins Regime (BoE)
Eligible Issuers
• Bank subsidiaries, federal nonbank licensees, or
state issuers with federal registration, with
enhanced oversight for >$10bn circulation.

• EMTs: EU-authorised credit institutions or authorised
electronic money institutions. Restrictions apply to
non-EU currency EMTs (exceeding MiCAR
thresholds) as a means of exchange
• FCA-authorised firms for non-systemic stablecoins,
• Systemic issuers to be designated by HM Treasury
and jointly regulated under BoE/FCA oversight
Balance Sheet
Treatment
• Stablecoins treated as reserve-backed digital
payment assets, segregated in bankruptcy-remote

accounts and treated as cash equivalents
• EMTs are fiat-referenced cryptoassets subject to
MiCAR and e-money requirements
• (OCC/FDIC) Mandates prudential capital buffers
tailored to issuer risk profile.
• (Fed) Framework consistent with bank requirements
• EMI issuers: minimum own funds of 2% of EMT
supply; 3% for significant EMTs. Significant issuers
subject to enhanced risk-management requirements
• Systemic: 6 months operating expenses/largest

plausible loss event
• Non-systemic: 1% av. stablecoins in circulation
Capital Reserve
Requirements**

AUTHORS
Marion Laboure is a Managing Director and Macro Strategist at Deutsche Bank in London, and a Lecturer in Economics and Finance at
Harvard University.
She was previously an economist at the IMF, the Central Bank of Luxembourg, Barclays, and the European Commission. She has twice
received the First Prize of the American Society of Actuaries, was named a rising star in finance by Revue Banque, and is recognised

among the 45 standout women in fintech globally.
She holds a Bachelor's degree in Mathematics from the University of Paris Dauphine, a Master of Research in Economics from the
London School of Economics, and a PhD in Econometrics and Quantitative Economics from the École Normale Supérieure.
She is the author of Democratizing Finance published by Harvard University Press.
Camilla Siazon is an Associate on Deutsche Bank’s Macro and Thematic team in London.

She focuses on digital assets, geopolitics, and global macroeconomic trends. Prior to Deutsche Bank, she worked at geopolitical
advisory firms Eurasia Group and Greenmantle.
She holds a dual MA/MSc in International History from Columbia University and the London School of Economics, and a BA in History
from Barnard College, Columbia University.

### Page 2

The Week Ahead October 2, 2026 
                                                                                                                                         2

CENTRAL BANK MEETINGS

India: RBI monetary policy committee 
meeting (7-Oct)

The macro environment has undergone 
significant changes since the RBI 
monetary policy committee’s (MPC) 
August policy meeting, with global oil 
prices rising past $100bl, and US Fed 
kickstarting their rate hike cycle. 
Domestically, core and headline 
continued to rise, laying the ground for 
inflation to quicken beyond 5.8 -6.0% in 
the second half as price risks broaden. 
Strong 1QFY27 GDP growth meanwhile 
pointed to the economy’s relative 
resilience to external shocks.

Besides fuel feeding into food costs, 
weather has also proved to be disruptive, 
with indications that the El Niño 
phenomenon could extend into 2027. 
International food benchmarks, including 
the UN Food Price index and the 
Bloomberg spot agricultural indice s, 
continue to climb. India’s food inflation 
has become more broad -based, shifting 
beyond vegetables and pulses to sugar, 
milk, protein groups, and edible oils. Sub-
par weather has contributed to this 
upmove, besides idiosyncratic factors 
like swing in production trends (cobweb), 
diversion for fuel usage etc.

After two years of normal monsoon, 
cumulative southwest rainfall this year is 
set to end with rainfall around 12 -13% 
below the long -term average, the most 
since 2015 and resulting in a slower 
build-up in reservoir levels.  
Global benchmark oil price gauge has 
averaged ~$93pb in FY27 year -to-date, 
up 36% yoy  vs FY26. Retail pump fuel 
prices were adjusted in quick succession 
in May26 and have been left unchanged 
since then, while other fuel products 
including CNG, aviation fuel, LPG etc 
have continued to climb. Add to this, 
other pockets like telecom, restaura nts, 
business services, transport fares etc 
have been inching up, reflecting early 
signs of broadening in price pressures (on 
sequential basis). A gradual broadening 
of price pressures is likely to keep 
headline inflation high and narrow the 
real rate buffer significantly.

Authorities are also conducting liquidity 
absorption measures through market 
operations like VRRR, open market 
operations and sell-buy FX swaps. This is 
likely to align the overnight call money 
rates to the benchmark repo rate, after 
having fallen sharply d ue to the surplus 
in the banking system.  Liquidity and core 
inflation have historically displayed a 
modest positive relationship, as per our 
study. As such, the RBI's inflation 
mandate has likely reinforced the push to 
drain excess liquidity.

We expect the RBI MPC to raise the repo 
rate by 25bp to 5.5% , citing pipeline 
inflationary pressures. Stance could be 
changed to withdrawal of 
accommodation to signal hawkish intent. 
We expect one more hike within 4Q26, 
though the odds of additional hikes have 
risen as oil prices stay stubbornly high 
and weather remains unfavourable.

FORTHCOMING DATA 
RELEASES

Malaysia

We expect industrial production to grow 
by 5.8% yoy in August from 4.7% yoy in 
July. Strength in  export-oriented 
manufacturing is likely to persist , 
supported by sustained demand for 
artificial intelligence-related electronics 
goods, while mining output may have 
recovered from its contraction in July.

Taiwan

September trade and inflation data are 
forthcoming. Export growth is expected 
to sustain the 40% pace seen in the 
previous month. The global AI boom is 
facing rising headwinds from 
synchronised rate hikes by G3 central 
banks, higher bond yields, and rising  
financing costs. That said, there are so 
far no clear signs of a significant 
slowdown, based on the high -frequency 
indicators we track across AI demand, 
investment, and supply.

CPI is expected to rebound to 2.4% YoY 
in September, up from 2.0% in August. 
The earlier arrival of the Mid -Autumn 
Festival this year may have boosted food 
and services prices on a YoY basis. The 
rebound in global energy prices should 
also have lifted tran sport costs, 
including gasoline prices and airfares. 
While electricity tariffs will remain 
frozen, the December review implies a 
possibility of an electricity price hike 
after the local elections.

Given still -strong economic growth, 
persistent inflation, and the potential for 
inflation expectations to rise, we 
maintain our view that the central bank 
will raise the policy rate by a modest 
12.5bps to 2.125% in December.

Thailand

We expect Thailand’s headline inflation 
to accelerate to 3.2% yoy in September 
from 2.5% yoy  in August, slightly 
exceeding the central bank’s 1 -3% 
inflation target. Faster increases in 
energy and food prices likely pushed 
overall inflation higher, despite an 
easing in electricity tariffs starting 
September. We expect the Bank of 
Thailand (BOT), w hich views the rise in 
inflation as largely supply -driven, to 
keep its policy rate stable at 1.00% for 
the remainder of 2026, aiming to support 
an economy with low and uneven growth.

Charts of the week: Diverging volatility

Strong earnings should resolve the bond and equity market divergence; French political risks remain largely contained  
S&P500 volatility index (VIX); US bond volatility index (MOVE)*   10-year government bond yield spreads over German bonds

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; *measures US government bond volatility

Editorial 
Will earnings resolve the bond-equity split?  
Strategy summary:  Bond and equity market volatility has 
sharply diverged. Elevated bond volatility and political risks in 
the US and Europe warrant near-term caution, prompting us to 
lock in tactical gains in semiconductors and Taiwan equities. 
Our quantitative stock-bond model turned negative for the first 
time since March 2025, signalling near-term equity volatility . 
We stay invested in a diversified allocation  slightly overweight 
equities and prefer to navigate the upcoming results and macro 
events before adding high-beta exposure within equities. 
Consensus estimates point to a c. 31% y/y rise in S&P500 Q3 
earnings, driven by technology -sector earnings growth of c. 
67%. Earnings have beaten lofty estimates in H1 as the AI 
build-out exceeds expectations. This favours tech -led equities 
in the medium term. In Europe, French political and fiscal risks 
cloud the EUR outlook, but regional bank equities remain 
attractive on robust profitability, capital strength and valuations. 
Divergence between equities and bonds.  Equity markets 
signal calm, with the S&P 500 index near record highs and its 
volatility index (VIX) low at 15, while the US government bond 
volatility index (MOVE) is close to its highest since March. Bond 
stress reflects two-decade-high long yields amid strong growth, 
oil close to USD 100/bbl fuelling near -term inflation, fiscal 
uncertainty and crowded investor short positioning vulnerable 
to abrupt unwinds. High bond volatility could spill into equities. 
Our quantitative stock-bond model turns negative. For the 
first time since March 2025, the model score has turned 
negative, driven by deteriorating equity market breadth and 
above-average risk aversion. History shows global equity 
volatility rises most in the month after the score dips to -1. 
However, equities still outperformed bonds over the following 
1-12 months, on average, despite higher market risk. 
Taking some chips off the table; locking in gains:  Rising 
bond volatility, the negative stock -bond model signal, weak 
equity breadth and approaching US mid-term elections warrant 
near-term caution. US small -caps remain about 9% below 
August’s all-time highs even as large-caps reach new records. 
The macro uncertainty prompted us to lock in gains in 
semiconductor and Taiwan equity opportunistic ideas. 
Strong Q3 earnings season likely to overcome near -term 
challenges. Strong US and Asian earnings should ultimately 
resolve the equity -bond divergence in favour of stocks. 
Consensus S&P 500 Q3 earnings growth has risen to 30 .6% 
y/y, while technology is tracking 66.5%, driven by the AI 
infrastructure buildout. AI investment plans, monetisation and 
funding needs are key metrics to watch. Record memory -chip 
profits and higher long -term targets from AI leaders support 
staying long technology-led risk assets and using near -term 
volatility to scale into preferred sectors. Also, US equities have 
historically performed strongly in the 12 months after mid -term 
elections, particularly when the opposition regains control of the 
House and the Senate – as seems increasingly likely. 
Medium-term constructive on equities. Beyond Q3 earnings, 
we expect US earnings to rise 15-20% next year. Almost half of 
the estimated USD 420 per share of S&P500 earnings in 2027 
should come from tech-related sectors: information technology, 
communication services and consumer discretionary. We also 
favour US, Europe and Japan financial sectors as solid growth 
and higher rates lift lending income, while the AI buildout boosts 
advisory fees from IPOs and M&A s. However, a nother 30-
50bps rise in long-term bond yields , without earnings growth 
upgrades, could be a  headwind for equities.  Next week’s US 
inflation data for September is the next catalyst for bond yields. 
French uncertainty challenges near -term EUR view; 
continue to see opportunities in Euro area bank equities.  
Rising French political and fiscal risks have largely remained 
confined to France, with its bond yield spread versus German 
peers surging to the widest since the 2011-12 Euro area crisis. 
Nevertheless, EUR/USD has fallen around 4% since mid -
August, and our three-month forecast of 1.15 is under review. 
Tighter financial conditions and political uncertainty in France 
and Spain raises the risk of a n ECB rate hold. Euro area bank 
equities remain attractive on profitability, capital strength and 
valuations, subject to sovereign-spread and funding-cost risks.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya and Sundeep Gantori

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 9 October 2026

Top client questions 
  What are the market implications of France's political and 
fiscal uncertainty?   
Our view: France’s political and fiscal uncertainty remain near-
term headwinds for European assets. We are placing our three-
month EUR/USD forecast (1.15) under review for a potential 
downward adjustment, while retaining our 12-month forecast at 
1.18 as broader Euro  area spillovers remain limited. Our base 
case remains one more ECB hike in December followed by a 
prolonged pause, although tighter financial conditions increase 
the risk of a delay. For equities, we retain Euro area banks as 
an Opportunistic idea, supported by strong profitability, capital 
buffers and reasonable valuations. For bank bonds, we prefer a 
subordinated capital structure to senior debt. 
Rationale: France’s 10-year government bond yield has risen amid 
renewed political and fiscal concerns. This has tightened financial 
conditions and added to the near-term EUR risk premium. Together 
with elevated US yields, this raises downside risks to our three-
month EUR/USD view. However, the stress has not yet developed 
into broad Euro area contagion, and recent expectations of progress 
on France’s 2027 budget have provided some relief. Broader Euro  
area growth and labour  market fundamentals also remain relatively 
resilient, supporting our unchanged 12-month EUR/USD view. 
For the ECB, higher sovereign yields are already delivering some 
monetary tightening. Markets have consequently pared expectations 
of additional ECB hikes. We continue to expect one more hike in 
December, given elevated near -term inflation, but persistent fiscal 
or political stress in France and Spain could delay further tightening. 
For Euro area banks, wider sovereign spreads  are likely to  raise 
funding costs and pressure bond portfolios, while fewer ECB hikes 
would moderate further gains in net interest income. Nevertheless, 
profitability remains strong, capital buffers are healthy and price-
to-book valuations remain reasonable, with returns on equity also 
improving. We, therefore, maintain Euro area banks as an 
Opportunistic idea , while monitoring sovereign spreads, funding 
costs and any deterioration in loan-loss provisions.  
On the bond side, European bank junior subordinated bond prices 
fell by an average of 3-5 cash points before rebounding, with French 
bank subordinated bonds underperforming those of other European 
peers. As fundamentals remain strong, we view pullbacks as 
opportunities to add and maintain our preference for a subordinated 
capital structure over senior debt for European bank bonds. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
— Fook Hien Yap, Senior Investment Strategist  
 Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist

France’s fiscal concerns weigh on the EUR

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is the likelihood of elevated long -end government 
bond yields evolving into a systemic risk for the financial sector 
and public finances? 
Our view: We believe s ystemic risk remains a tail risk rather 
than a base case in the near term. The probability would rise 
materially if rollover stress, foreign demand withdrawal and 
private credit deterioration occur in combination. 
Rationale: Higher bond yields worsen fiscal deficits and drive higher 
issuance requirements, exerting further upward pressure on yields. 
The resulting tighter financial conditions weigh on economic growth. 
This feedback loop is most acute in Developed Markets (DMs) where 
debt burdens are already elevated. For instance, projected 2026 
debt-to-GDP ratios stand at 204% for Japan, 138% for Italy, 126% 
for the US, 118% for France and 104% for the UK. Countries with 
shorter average debt maturities face the sharpest near-term 
repricing risk  as maturing obligations are refinanced at current 
rates. Meanwhile, evidence suggests that  a positive equity-bond 
correlation increases the sensitivity of yields to bond supply.  
Taking the US as an example, there has been no buyers’ strike on 
US government bonds yet , though foreign demand has softened, 
narrowing the margin of safety. The US government has shifted 
towards shorter-dated issuance to manage funding costs (reducing 
near-term expenses, but concentrating rollover risk). Economic 
resilience remains evident, underpinned by corporate confidence 
in AI -driven investment . The key question is whether this 
expansion can persist long enough to stabilise the fiscal trajectory. 
Within the financial sector, corporate balance sheets  remain 
strong, while High-Yield (HY) bond and leveraged loan default rates 
remain contained, though this reflects the lag between rate rises and 
credit deterioration, not immunity. Following the failure of the Silicon 
Valley Bank, liquidity discipline among banks has improved, but 
duration mismatches persist.  
In private credit, default rates are trending upward while the asset 
class has come under continued scrutiny , with negative headlines 
and a rise in redemptions trigger ing fund gating  at some  private 
credit managers since Q 1 2026. Hence, continued monitoring of 
default rates, redemptions and potential stress events in private 
credit remains essential . That said, higher rates could buoy 
medium-term returns and distribution yields in the asset class , 
as the majority of direct lending loans have a floating-rate structure. 
—  Ray Heung, Senior Investment Strategist

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 9 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is your outlook for the US Q3 2026 earnings season, 
especially for the banking sector? 
Our view: We expect another quarter of robust US earnings 
growth to support our Overweight stance on US equities. We 
see m ajor US banks, which begin reporting next week, also 
delivering healthy results, reinforcing our Overweight view on  
the US financial sector. 
Rationale: Consensus forecasts S&P500 Q3 earnings growth  of 
30.6% y/y, up from 27.6% at the start of July. Energy is expected to 
post the strongest growth at 114.7%, supported by elevated oil 
prices amid ongoing geopolitical tensions. Information technology 
(66.5%) and communication services (49.0%) remain key 
earnings drivers, benefiting from sustained AI -related demand and 
investment. The broader outlook remains supportive, with full-year 
earnings growth projected at 35.8% in 2026 and 15.6% in 2027. 
For banks, the recent underperformance amid concerns over higher 
long-term bond yields has, in our view, improved the forward-looking 
risk-reward profile.  Over the medium term, strengthening loan 
growth and resilient credit quality should continue to support solid 
fundamentals. Capital markets activity also remains constructive, 
with industry leaders well -positioned to benefit from their strong 
investment banking and trading franchises. 
—  Jason Wong, Senior Equity Analyst 
  How could upcoming semiconductor manufacturing and 
equipment earnings reshape the AI capex outlook? 
Our view: We maintain our AI capex outlook as robust demand 
and ongoing supply constraints support a multi-year expansion 
cycle. In the near term, however, we take a more prudent stance 
on semiconductors amid an expected rise in equity volatility. 
Rationale: Recent memory industry guidance points to sustained 
investment in advanced chip manufacturing , with customer 
commitments extending through 2031. Upcoming earnings should 
provide visibility into capacity expansion plans. This supports our AI 
capex outlook, which we will monitor through the earnings season.  
That said, we expect equity volatility to rise in the near term amid 
elevated long-term yields and macroeconomic uncertainty. Recent 
media reports on AI lab revenues  have also led to doubts over 
monetisation and the ability to sustain elevated capex spending. We 
thus took profit on our Global Semiconductors (8.1% return from 9-
Sep-26 to 2-Oct-26) and MSCI Taiwan (10.0% return from 9-Jul-26 to 
2-Oct-26) Opportunistic ideas following strong performance. This does 
not reflect a deterioration in fundamentals, but rather a need to be 
prudent on high-beta exposure. We remain constructive on the AI 
theme over the long term. 
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 9 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  USD/CHF has rebounded sharply over the past two 
months. What are the key factors behind the move, and do you 
see the currency pair continuing to move higher? 
Our view:  USD/CHF tested 0.84 amid strong momentum from 
the USD, but we see the pair remain ing supported near -term 
before gradually moving lower towards 0.82 in three months. 
Rationale: The Fed’s relatively high policy rate continues to support 
USD carry demand , while the Swiss National Bank maintains its 
policy rate at 0%. In the near term, however, the Swiss franc (CHF) 
could benefit from increased safe-haven demand amid mounting 
fiscal concerns in France  and political uncertainty in Spain , 
particularly if European risk premia continue to rise. Beyond these 
cyclical factors, Switzerland’s substantial net foreign asset position, 
persistent current account surpluses and low public debt burden 
continue to underpin the CHF’s structural strength . Over the 
medium term, we expect the USD’s rate advantage to gradually 
diminish as the Fed approaches the end of its tightening cycle, while 
the CHF’s defensive characteristics should remain supportive.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist 
  Do you see further upside for USD/JPY after recent dovish 
comments from BoJ officials and latest bond auctions? 
Our view: We see some near-term upside risk to USD/JPY from 
elevated US  yields, but retain our medium -term constructive 
JPY view. USD/JPY remains below 159, suggesting markets are 
mindful of intervention risk as the pair approaches 160. We 
continue to expect further BoJ normalisation. 
Rationale: Recent BoJ communication appears less dovish than the 
market narrative had suggested. BoJ Governor Ueda continues to 
emphasise the need to anchor underlying inflation around 2%  
and gradually reduce monetary accommodation, supporting our view 
that the tightening cycle is not yet over. An October pause would 
reflect a more measured pace of policy normalisation  rather than a 
change in the BoJ’s policy direction. 
Recent Japanese government bond (JGB) auctions have shown 
resilient demand despite higher yields.  At the latest 10 -year JGB 
auction, the bid -to-cover ratio improved  to 3.76x from 3.29x 
previously, while the auction tail (spread between highest 
accepted yield and issued yield) narrowed to 0.2bps from 1.6bps. 
We see this as indicative of an orderly, functioning market rather than 
a dovish BoJ signal . Intervention concerns around  the 160 level 
should help limit near-term USD/JPY upside.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

資料來源：臺灣指數公司，國泰證期研究部整理

王筠婷 / 特用化學、半導體週邊 
+886-2-2326-9899#7961 
vanessawang@cathayfut.com.tw

弘塑 3131 TT  ( 電子上游–設備 ) 
 ) 
訂單能見度無虞 
投資與評價 
1. 弘塑 2Q26 毛利率僅達 38.9%， 低於我們原先及市場預期的40%水準，
主要受到添鴻產品結構轉差及新廠折舊攤提影響。 
2. 3Q26 受惠於 CoWoS 等機台認列數成長及強勁拉貨需求，營收達
22.21 億元；研究部預估 EPS 為 15.86 元。 
3. FY26 全年出機量預估維持在 220 至 240 台，展望至 FY27/FY28，
FY27 設備出貨量預估將年增 50%，FY28 亦有機會再成長 50%。 
4. 研究部預估 FY26/27 EPS 分別為 60.66 元/96.33 元，維持弘塑買進投
資建議，目標價 2,500 元，以 26x FY27 獲利預估。 
重點更新 
至 FY28 出貨量能將持續成長 
研究部認為弘塑設備出貨展望能見度將能延伸至 FY28。 由於晶圓廠 、OSAT、
記憶體客戶皆對先進封裝設備需求相當迫切，設備交期已長達 10 至 12 個
月，惟受限於短期人力供應與廠房籌備進度， FY26 全年出機量預估維持在 
220 至 240 台。展望至 FY27/FY28，FY27 設備出貨量預估將年增 50%，
FY28 亦有機會再成長 50%。 
添鴻化學品業務將於 1Q27 逐漸看見轉機 
隨 OSAT 客戶於今年 Q2/Q3 進入裝機密集期，新產線仍須經測試與量產爬
坡，相關的化學品需求通常會在設備交機後約 3~4 個月逐步提升，因此短期
營收仍受新廠折舊與稼動率偏低影響，預期添鴻的整體稼動率與毛利率有望
在 1Q27 逐漸擺脫谷底，迎來較為明顯的營運轉機。 
訂單能見度無虞 
展望 2026 年至 2028 年 ， 在各先進封裝平台強勁需求下 ， 設備產能已告全
滿，交期長達 10 至 12 個月，訂單能見度直達 2H27。就產品組合來看，
設備業務高階產品比重將逐漸提升，預估至 FY27 SoIC /CPO 等營收占比將
提升至 10~15%，添鴻化學品也將受惠於 OSAT 客戶逐漸提升拉貨動能，整
體營收與獲利成長動能明確。研究部預估 FY26/27 EPS 分別為 60.66 元
/96.33 元(YoY＋33.5%/+58.8%)。弘塑目前交易於 35/22 倍之 2026/2027 
年獲利預估。研究部維持買進投資建議，目標價 2,500 元，以 26x FY27 獲
利預估。

### Page 2

訪談內容 
3Q26 受惠於機台認列數成長，營收呈現季增 
弘塑 2Q26 營收 17.27 億元 (QoQ +8.2% / YoY +5.9%)，符合我們原先預估的 16.88 億元；毛
利率僅達 38.9% (QoQ +5.1ppts / YoY -1.7ppts)，低於我們原先及市場預期的 40%水準，主要
受到添鴻產品結構轉差及新廠折舊攤提影響 ；EPS 為 11.18 元 (QoQ -30.6% / YoY +58.6%)，
略低於我們原先預期的 11.73 元。 
3Q26 受惠於 CoWoS 等機台認列數成長及強勁拉貨需求，營收呈現季增，營收達 22.21 億元
(QoQ +28.7%/YoY+48.7%) ；研究部預估毛利率 略為季增至 40.1% (QoQ + 1.2ppts / YoY 
-0.5ppts)，產品組合持續改善，預估 EPS 為 15.86 元(QoQ +41.9%/ YoY +52.5%)。 
展望 4Q26， 受惠於先進封裝及記憶體等強勁需求的持續出機認列，研究部預期營收將持續季增，
預估 4Q26 營收將達 24.87 億元(QoQ +12.0%/YoY+15.7%)，毛利率將持穩於 40.0% (QoQ 
-0.1ppts / YoY -2.1ppts)。整體而言，預估 FY26 全年營收達 80.32 億元(YoY+23.3%)，毛利率
達 38.5%(YoY-2.6 ppts)，EPS 為 60.66 元(YoY+33.5%)。

至 FY28 出貨量能將持續成長 
研究部認為弘塑設備出貨展望能見度將能延伸至 FY28。由於晶圓廠、OSAT、記憶體客戶皆對先
進封裝設備需求相當迫切，設備交期已長達 10 至 12 個月，惟受限於短期人力供應與廠房籌
備進度，FY26 全年出機量預估維持在 220 至 240 台。為突破產能瓶頸，新竹租賃廠房已展開
重新配置，預計於 2026 年底投產，同時公司亦積極評估購置新竹現成廠房。 
展望至 FY27/FY28，FY27 設備出貨量預估將年增 50%，FY28 亦有機會再成長 50%。至 FY27，
OSAT 客戶的設備訂單將較 2026 年呈現倍數成長，正式接棒成為 FY27 推升公司營運成長的最
大主力。至 FY28 及中長期，受惠於先進封裝發展趨勢明確，除出貨量增加外，SoIC、CPO 及
面板級封裝等封裝技術迭代帶動設備 ASP 顯著提升（SoIC 設備較 CoWoS 設備單價增加 30% 
至 50%，面板級封裝達 2.5 至 3 倍以上），外包比例亦有望因自家產能開出而下降，整體營運呈
現量價齊揚。

### Page 3

添鴻化學品業務將於 1Q27 逐漸看見轉機 
隨 OSAT 客戶於今年 Q2/Q3 進入裝機密集期，新產線仍須經測試與量產爬坡，相關的化學品需
求通常會在設備交機後約 3~4 個月逐步提升，因此短期營收仍受新廠折舊與稼動率偏低影響，
預期添鴻的整體稼動率與毛利率有望在 1Q27 逐漸擺脫谷底，迎來較為明顯的營運轉機。 
在設備出機量持續提升 ， 且化學品業務將於FY27 看見較大轉機 ，研究部預估 FY27 營收達 115.49
億元(YoY+43.8%)。利潤率將受惠於產品組合轉好、外包比例降低而提升，預估 FY27 全年毛利
率達 43.0%(YoY+4.5ppts)，全年 EPS 為 96.33 元(YoY+58.8%)。 
訂單能見度無虞 
展望 2026 年至 2028 年，在各先進封裝平台強勁需求下 ， 設備產能已告全滿 ， 交期長達 10 至 
12 個月，訂單能見度直達 2H27。就產品組合來看，設備業務高階產品比重將逐漸提升，預估
至 FY27 SoIC/CPO 等營收占比將提升至 10~15%， 添鴻化學品也將受惠於OSAT 客戶逐漸提升
拉貨動能，整體營收與獲利成長動能明確。 
研究部預估 FY26/27 EPS 分別為 60.66 元/96.33 元(YoY＋33.5%/+58.8%)。弘塑目前交易於
35/22 倍之 2026/2027 年獲利預估 。 研究部維持買進投資建議 ， 目標價2,500 元，以 26x FY27 
獲利預估。

公司簡介 
弘塑集團由四家公司組成： 2013 年合併化學品公司添鴻、 2015 年投資日本公司 T azmo/ 
Apprecia、2018 年合併佳霖、收購太引，並投資 SVXR。

### Page 4

ESG 
弘塑 ESG 報告書依循由全球永續性報告協會(Global Reporting Initiative, GRI) 所發布之永續性報
導準則(GRI Standards)之核心選項編撰，同時參考上市上櫃公司永 續發展實務守則及聯合國永續
發展目標，揭露永續發展相關資訊。報告書中所揭  露的統計數據皆由譜瑞各權責單位彙整提供。
財務績效數據係經資誠聯合會計師事務所依照國際財務報導準則 (International Financial 
Reporting Standards, IFRS)查核簽證之年度合併財務報告。 
除了開發具有競爭力的產品外，弘塑亦以實踐永續發展自期，對商業行為採取最  高專業及道德的
標準，善盡對利害關係人與環境的關心與責任，整合公司資源遵守永續性原則，並持續進行各項改
善。 
經濟面： 
A. 股東權益：公司優點為董事長親自出席股東常會；股東會結束後公開揭露逐項議案之詳細
表決資訊。法規遵循方面，公司於題項評估期間未有不合理或受主管機關處分之情事。(例
如：訴訟、未分派股利、未說明受理小股東提案期間、持股質押比逾 50%、庫藏股買回
或私募股票理由不合理之情形)。 
B. 董事會結構與運作：公司優點為公司未有政府機關、屬同集團之公司或財團法人占董事會
席次達三分之一；揭露審計委員會運作情形；針對功能性委員會進行績效評估，並揭露評
估結果。公司可加強處為法定外功能性委員會之獨立董事未達半數，或未揭露該委員會之
組成、職責與運作情形；董事會之外部績效評估頻率未達標準。獨立董事方面，公司優點
為至少 2 名獨立董事之連續任期不超過 9 年。董事會永續治理方面，公司優點為負責永
續發展或企業社會責任的最高負責人為總經理；當年度永續報告書經董事會通過。董事會
績效與薪酬方面，公司可加強處為未揭露董事薪酬與績效評估結果之連結；企業永續績效
尚未與高階主管及董事薪酬進行連結。董事會多元性方面，公司優點為揭露董事會多元化
政策並揭露落實情形；董事會成員具備不同專業背景。 
C. 行為準則與內控：公司優點為制定反貪腐政策；制定檢舉制度並揭露檢舉情形；訂定內部
規則禁止內部人利用公司內部訊息於有價證券市場獲利 。 公司可加強處為未揭露反競爭或
反托拉斯的相關政策。內控單位與人才方面，公司優點為設置誠信經營負責單位；設置公
司治理主管，並說明職權範圍、業務執行重點及進修情形；內部人員有取得內部稽核相關
證照，例如：內部稽核師。法規遵循方面，評估期間公司未違反重大誠信經營與內控準則
等公司治理相關法規。 
D. 風險及危機管理：公司優點為訂立各種類型的風險回應策略；考量營運中對價值鏈所產生
的環境及社會外部性並揭露相關作為。公司可加強處為未揭露風險管控專責單位；未說明
其風險政策經董事會通過。資訊安全方面，公司優點為設置資安長或資訊安全專責單位；
制定資訊管理政策；制定定期檢視、測試資訊安全機制，並說明檢視成果；提供員工資訊
安全相關教育訓練。公司可加強處為未取得資訊安全相關認證；未揭露個資相關政策。 
E. ESG 創新：公司可加強處為未揭露綠色相關部門設置情形與職責；未揭露綠色/永續採購
之政策或金額 。 此外 ， 產業創新方面 ， 公司優點為對其專利之智慧財產權進行管理與保護，
並揭露量化績效。法規遵循方面，公司近 3 年並未受公平交易委員會懲罰。

### Page 5

社會面： 
A. 人權：公司優點為評估可能遭遇的人權議題，及提出因應措施；揭露人權政策執行結果。
法規遵循方面，評估期間未違反人權相關法規。 
B. 員工溝通與福利：公司優點為定期召開勞資會議供員工參與討論其權利和義務；揭露優於
法規的員工休假福利；訂定具體之職業安全衛生政策且明確揭露實際成果；設有職業安全
衛生委員會負責工安事項；針對工傷事件，揭露統計數據；設定減少工安事件的目標。在
工會爭議與職業災害方面，未有重大職業災害情事。 
C. 人力資本發展：公司優點為制定員工績效評估辦法且定期評比；提供員工能力發展培育計
畫；揭露當年度員工訓練的投入時間或成本。公司可加強處為未透過指標衡量員工訓練成
果。人才留任方面，公司優點為揭露新進員工人數或其占總員工人數之比例，並依照不同
指標(如性別、年齡等)區分；揭露員工離職人數、離職率或流動率，並依照不同指標(如性
別、年齡等)區分；公司可加強處為未提及員工認股權、持股信託等股權分享計畫；未揭
露員工滿意度調查結果。 
D. 多元組成與包容性：公司優點為針對員工組成之多元性擬定策略與目標，並揭露員工組成
結構。公司可加強處為未揭露各階級男女薪酬比；未揭露女性主管人數或佔比；未說明少
數族群雇用情形。 
E. 身心障礙員工雇用方面，當年度足額雇用身心障礙員工或針對未足額情形提出改善措施。 
F. 供應鏈社會面向控管：公司優點為詳細說明人權政策包含項目；依循《 RBA 責任商業聯
盟準則》保障供應商或承攬商之人權。公司可加強處為未說明在新供應商遴選標準中的
ESG 社會面準則；未說明是否具負責任礦物採購管理流程， 或要求供應商簽屬相關衝突礦
產聲明書。 
G. 客戶關係管理：公司優點為公司透過專人或管道即時處理客戶之意見、揭露客訴情形及改
善措施；揭露客戶滿意度數據並設定客戶滿意度目標。法規遵循方面，評估期間並未違反
消費者權益相關爭議或法律訴訟，或已針對違反情形進行說明。 
H. 產品安全：公司優點為自行設立實驗室或委託第三方公正實驗室抽樣產品品質；制定產品
製程持續計畫，確保生產計劃不中斷；取得品質相關認證。公司可加強處為未揭露與產品
品質/服務品質相關之教育訓練時數或費用。 
環境面： 
A.  環境系統與治理：公司優點為回覆 CDP 問卷。公司可加強處為未揭露將綠色概念融入營
運過程的作為。 
B. 空氣管理：公司可加強處為未揭露空氣汙染管理專責單位及相關管理政策、未揭露空氣汙
染排放數據、未揭露量化空氣汙染減量目標。法規遵循方面，評估期間未違反空氣汙染相
關法規或已針對違反情形提出具體改善措施。

### Page 6

C. 能源與氣候變遷：公司優點為說明董事會或管理階層真對氣候變遷議題之監督情形；評估
氣候變遷機會。公司可加強處為未揭露使用具公信力的 1.5 °C 以下及 3 °C 以上溫度情
境評估氣候風險；未揭露與供應鏈或合作夥伴共同推動因應氣候變遷相關策略。溫室氣體
管理方面，公司優點為制定溫室氣體管理政策；揭露至少三年的溫室氣體範疇一與二排放
量，並揭露涵蓋範圍或佔營收比率；制定量化溫室氣體減量目標追蹤績效；通過碳盤查認
證；執行碳足跡制度。公司可加強處為未揭露範疇三排放量。節能管理方面，公司優點為
制定節能管理政策；揭露用電或節能數據；進行能源風險評估。公司可加強處為未揭露節
能量化目標。再生能源管理方面，公司優點為制定再生能源管理政策；公司可加強處為未
揭露再生能源使用量或發電量；未揭露量化再生能源發展目標。 
D. 水管理：公司優點為制定廢水管理政策；公司可加強處為未揭露量化廢汙水減量目標。水
資源管理方面，公司優點為制定水資源管理政策、揭露用水來源；公司可加強處為未揭露
量化用水減量目標。法規遵循方面，評估期間未違反水資源相關法規或已針對違反情形提
出具體改善措施。 
E. 原物料與廢棄物管理：公司優點為揭露廢棄物、資源回收總量。公司可加強處為未揭露原
物料使用量；未揭露量化廢棄物減量目標；未揭露量化有毒／有害廢棄物減量目標。法規
遵循方面 ， 評估期間未違反廢棄物及有害物質相關法規或已針對違反情形提出具體改善措
施。 
F. 供應商及產品生命週期管理：公司優點為制定綠色供應鏈管理政策。公司可加強處為公司
未揭露供應鏈環境管理成效。

緯穎 6669 TT(電子–電腦及週邊設備) 
GPU、ASIC 並進，27 年獲利創高 
投資與評價 
1. 因缺料影響降低，加上 Trainium3 開始出貨，緯穎 3Q26 營收優於預期
17%，上修獲利 25%至 EPS 43.9 元，3Q26 營收、獲利將同創新高。 
2. 在 ASIC、GPU 貢獻下，4Q26 營收估季增高個位數 ，AI 比重將逾 55~60%，
因 2H26 優於預期，上修今年營收 8%至 1.46 兆元，EPS 140.3 元。 
3. AMD Helios 為緯穎 27 年 AI 強勁動能，加上公司為 Trainium 水冷產品主
供，帶動 27 年稅後獲利 YoY+60%，CB 對股權稀釋僅 1.03%，EPS 估續
創新高達 224.71 元，維持買進評等，目標價 2,400 元。 
重點更新 
3Q26 營收、獲利上修，反映一般伺服器雙位數季增及液冷 ASIC 加入貢獻 
緯穎 3Q26 營收 4,346 億元，QoQ+56%、YoY+63%，優於研究部預期 17%，
主要因缺料影響較預期低，加上 9 月 AWS Trainium3 開始出貨，因本季一般伺
服器占比超越 50%，估毛利率維持 8.53%，稅後獲利上修 25%至 245.95 億元，
YoY+59%至 EPS 43.9 元。預期 3Q26 營收、獲利將同創新高。  
4Q26 AI 比重攀升至 55%以上，ASIC、GPU 同步貢獻，上修今年營收 8% 
研究部估緯穎 4Q26 營收將季增高個位數，AI 部分，ASIC 出貨 Trainium3 液冷
貢獻，ASP 估較氣冷版翻倍成長；此外，GPU 部分，預期 AMD Helios 及 Oracle 
Vera Rubin 皆有少量出貨，加上缺料持續改善，估 AI 比重將超越 55~60%，使
毛利率下滑至 8.2%，稅後獲利 YoY+81%，EPS 44.5 元。因 2H26 狀況優於預
期，上修今年營收 8%至 1.46 兆元 ，YoY+54%，反映下半年 AI 比重攀升至 55%，
毛利率 8.38%，稅後獲利 YoY+54%，EPS 140.34 元。 
27 年營運在 ASIC、GPU 成長增速下，獲利將再創新高 
展望 2027 年，預期 AMD Helios 將為 AI 強勁動能，2027 年 AMD GPU 機櫃估
達五到六千櫃 ，ASP 500 萬美元 ， 加上VR 機櫃貢獻 ， 估使GPU 占比拉升至 30%。
ASIC 部分，我們認為緯穎仍是 Trainium 水冷產品主供，預期 AWS 水冷比重大
幅攀升，估AWS 營收年增四到五成；一般伺服器維持兩成以上增長。綜上，上修
27 年營收 11%至 2.64 兆元，YoY+80.4 %，因 AI 占比估達六到七成，估毛利率
下滑至 7.35%，稅後獲利 YoY+60%，EPS 224.71 元，獲利續創新高。籌資部分，
緯穎將於 11 月完成 150 億元國內可轉債發行，預計在 1Q27~2Q27 分別使用 
80 億元、70.75 億元，對於整體股權稀釋僅 1.03%，影響有限。

呂旻旂/紡織、製鞋、伺服器 
+886-2-7718-7960/minchilu@cathayfut.com.tw

台灣央行利率連十凍，川習會前瞻，
高利環境與期中選舉下的新亞操作
分析師／吳雨桑
研究範疇／新興市場 / 新興亞洲 / 中國 / 香港/ 台灣
新興亞洲投資攻略
出 刊 日 : 2026/9/24
熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
市場投資展望
資產 4Q26看法 投資展望
中國兩年期殖利率 1.25% 景氣下行壓力持續，本行仍預期接下來有降準50bp空間。
新興市場股 中立 油價高檔拉鋸，主要國家央行貨幣政策偏向緊縮，債務堆壘隱憂揮之不去，高利環境下，兼具基本面穩健與政策操作有空間的新亞、拉美相對正向。
新興亞洲 中立 新興亞洲中相對看好台灣、韓國、東協與印度，AI推論的供不應求，適時留意評價面空間循序參與台韓投資契機，印度有評價面保護優勢。

中國A 中立 中國景氣K型化加劇，投資方向維持選股不選市，聚焦受惠AI相關供應鏈兼國產替代政策扶植的科技族群、稀缺原物料及高附加出口價值等產業。
香港 中立 削價競爭雖告尾聲但內需乏力影響獲利，並關注資本支出轉為獲利進程。
台灣 正向 長線台股AI供應鏈握技術進程優勢與漲價潮，擇評價面分批布局台股。

《投資關鍵-修正過後資金重拾AI投資議題》
9月全球股市歷經主要國家貨幣政策轉趨鷹派，市場
對政府債務堆壘的疑慮 ，主要國家公債殖利高檔拉
鋸影響對風險性資產的偏好 。惟歷經適度修正後 ，
市場重拾對AI投資主題的信心 ，台韓股市肩負重要
AI基礎建設的供應鏈要角 ，評價調整後重新吸引資
金進駐，在全球股市震盪之際中表現相對亮眼。

重點摘要
3
• 8月中國經濟數據仍顯示
外貿強 ，內需疲軟的景
象。除反應信心不足 、
內需疲軟外 ，反內卷的
制約以及政策傾斜高端
科技是主因。
• 中國經濟現階段來自價
格為主的出口貢獻 ，景
氣越趨依賴全球科技週
期，內需疲軟將視財政
執行力道進程才能緩解
接下來的景氣下行壓力 。
《投資關鍵-中國經濟K型化情勢依舊》
縱觀8月中國經濟數據仍顯示外貿強，內需疲軟的景
象。中國8月出口增速從前月23.9%回升至25%，究
其細項，科技相關以名目計算年增速續保高速 ，如
自動資料處理機器零件年增76.5%， 儲存組件年增
159.2%，印刷電路板年增 44.4%與積體電路年增
129.8%。惟若以數量計算年增大多回落，顯示科技

8月出口增速佳來自價格貢獻。另一留意低端消費出
口也見改善，可觀察歐美消費旺季的挹注力道。
8月零售銷售持續疲軟年增回落至 0.4%，汽車零售
年衰退幅度擴大至-18.5%是主要拖累，零組件價格
上揚帶動通訊設備零售增27.3%。
8月實質工業生產年增略好預期至 5.2%，科技相關
保持較高能見度 ，通訊、電腦及其他電子設備年增
17.2%，但如汽車 、粗鋼與水泥較反應內需表現黯
淡，反內卷政策制約太陽能板等生產表現 。固定資
產投資年衰退幅度擴大至 -7.2%，房地產投資雙位
數衰退趨勢不變 ，製造業投資年減 -2.3%，我們認
為除反應信心不足 、內需疲軟外 ，反內卷的制約以
及政策傾斜高端科技是主因。
總體來看，中國經濟現階段來自價格為主的出口貢
獻，景氣越趨依賴全球科技週期 ，內需疲軟將視財
政執行力道進程才能緩解接下來的景氣下行壓力。
出口雖穩健，但內需與投資乏力，產能利用不振

《投資關鍵-台灣連十季利率不動 ，但升息壓力增 》
台灣央行第三季理監事會議宣布重貼現率、擔保放
款融通利率與短期融通利率維持不變在 2.0%、
2.375%和4.25%；可轉讓定期存單 (NCD) 28天期、
91 天期 、 182 天 期 也 維 持1.215% 、 1.275% 和
1.395% 不變。2024年以來，台灣央行持續保持利
率十連凍紀錄。
不動產信用管制部分 ，由於截至7月底，全體銀行
不動產貸款集中度續降至34.44%，低於2020年12

月底(第1度調整信用管制時點 )之 36.64%)，因此
調升自然人第 2戶購屋貸款最高成數上限至 7成。
同時也刪除購地貸款有關切結於一定期間內動工興
建之規定，相關規範回歸銀行依授信實務辦理。
資料來源：台灣央行，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/09/18。

重點摘要
6
• 本行認為台灣景氣動能佳
且增長更加均衡 ，形成經
濟的正向循環 ，台灣經濟
環境已有宏觀的升息條件 。
年以來相較各國重返寬鬆貨幣政策列車 ，台灣依
舊維持基準利率 2%，2024/7各類新台幣存款準
備率調升 1碼。另觀察台灣 7月M2年增速 7.42%
(今年以來6.6%)雖高於政策目標區間 2.5%~6.5%，
但低於17.8%的名目 GDP 增長率，意味貨幣需求
增長速度超過貨幣供應量 ，因此台灣貨幣政策環
境已偏緊縮，所以並不需要立即升息。
展望後市，隨AI應用擴散至推論 、代理及終端裝
置推升算力需求 ，AI投資建設的暢旺驅動相關硬
體升級與用量成長 ，台灣企業涵蓋重要 AI供應鏈
節點，強勁出口引領經濟成長。

觀察AI科技紅利也逐步外溢其他產業 ，非科技如
機械出口逐步加溫 。相關半導體投資擴產需求積
極，投資成長也助力台灣經濟 ，內需也因股市財
富效應帶動。台灣景氣動能佳且增長更加均衡 ，
形成經濟的正向循環。
資料來源：主計處，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/08/07。
AI科技紅利外溢，消費與投資對景氣貢獻逐步擴大
本行認為台灣經濟環境已有宏觀的升息條件 ，而
從央行聲明觀察對於左右貨幣政策的觀察事項 ，
分別為國內通膨發展 ，主要經濟體貨幣政策緊縮
程度，國內金融情勢 、其他不確定性如 AI應用前
景。相較先前，央行將通膨發展情勢列為首要抉
擇貨幣政策指標。本行認為近期台灣的通膨情勢
對台灣經濟成長yoy貢獻 %

7
• 從央行聲明觀察對於左右
貨幣政策的觀察事項來看 ，
1Q27物價高於預期可能
性加大，年底主要經濟體

預期12月央行理監事會議
有 可 能 決 議 升 息 半 碼 至
2.125%。
並非主要來自供給測壓力，主要是考量核心CPI 已
連續四個月維持在 2%以上，搭配民間消費對 GDP

的貢獻逐季顯現 ，這表明持續的內需復甦正在產
生需求側通膨壓力 。另一方面根據台灣行政院院
長表示 ，普發現金經費已納入總預算 ，立法院預
算審查進度將定奪普發現金 1萬的時點，目前可能
的時間最快在2027農曆春節（2月4日至10日）前
後，抑或在元宵節（2月20日）前。而根據央行預
估1Q27整體物價增速仍高於 2%，若疊加現金補
貼的加持 ，不排除物價增幅將更高於原預期水準 。
其次央行聲明第二個觀察重點 : 主要經濟體貨幣政
策緊縮程度。美歐日央行9月已升息一碼，美聯準
會年底尚有升息一碼的可能性 ，韓國央行 7、8月
連續性升息後，11月與1Q27都有可能再度升息。
多國相繼貨幣政策緊縮態勢 ，或增加台灣央行貨
幣政策抉擇的壓力 。而若台灣基準利率與他國利
差擴大 ，也可能引發資金外流 ，也不利央行關心
的金融情勢發展 。因此綜合來看 ，本行預期 12月
央行理監事會議有可能決議升息半碼至2.125%。
《投資關鍵-聚焦穩健基本面有望抵禦高利環境》
展望後市，美財政部有意擴大美債回購規模緩和債

務壓力，美聯準會9月升息一碼至3.75%-4.0%，年
底不排除還有一碼的升息空間，但從通膨與景氣預
估路徑來看，美聯準會的升息舉措並非典型的升息
循環，而是「不承諾路徑的風險管理式升息」。
回到對新興亞洲貨幣政策路徑來看，或許促使部分
新興亞洲央行政策光譜往緊縮端推移，但不改變原
具降息需求新亞國家的寬鬆方向，整體新亞國家貨
幣政策走向分歧。更重要的是決定新興亞洲貨幣政
策的考量點，如台灣與韓國主要是因應景氣回溫而
貨幣政策緊縮，但像無財政補貼物價致緊縮貨幣政
策國家如菲律賓，再者還有經濟成動能尚不穩固或
通膨可控，貨幣政策按兵不動的如中國、泰國等。

## Document 456: ここが知りたい『投資信託販売チャネルの変化と現在地』

Source PDF: all report pdf/ここが知りたい『投資信託販売チャネルの変化と現在地』.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 1
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

投資信託販売チャネルの変化と現在地

資産運用調査部　主席研究員　大木 剛（おおき つよし）
　個人の資産運用において、 投資信託はNISAによる資
産形成やリタイアメン ト資金の運用などで、 重要性を増し
ている。 投資信託の販売では、 1998年12月の銀行窓販
解禁から四半世紀以上が経過した。 ここでは、 1999年以
降の公募投資信託の販売チャネル別の純資産額 （販売 ・
解約 ・ 時価変動が反映） と構成比率の推移を資料 １ で確認
したい。
　銀行窓販の解禁から1年後の1999年末時点で、 証券
は46.4兆円 （比率90％） 、 銀行等は2.8兆円 （同5％） とな
り、 その後も銀行等は預金取引等を背景とする顧客基盤
を活かし、 2007年末には34.0兆円 （同43％） まで比率
を高めた。 一方、 証券は45.3兆円 （同57％） と比率が低
下した。
　もっ とも、 銀行等の比率は2007～2008年をピークに
低下し、 証券の比率は上昇に転じた。 証券の純資産額に
は機関投資家も保有するETF （上場投資信託） が含まれ、
2010年代以降は日本銀行による大量買入れも統計上の
証券比率を大きく押し上げた。 資料 １ ・ ２ では、 個人向け投
資信託の動きをみるためETFを除いており、 それでも
2026年8月末時点では、 証券は159.9兆円 （同70％） 、 銀
行等は67.2兆円 （同29％） となっている。
投資信託の銀行窓販は証券にどれだけ迫ったか
　2009年以降の証券の比率上昇には、 証券各社の取組
みに加え、 いくつかの要因が考えられる。
　まず、 近年はNISAなどを活用した投資信託積立の拡大
により、 資産形成層を中心に大手オンライン証券会社の
利用が大きく増えている。
　また、 メガバンク等では、 顧客層に応じたグループ内の
銀行と証券の役割分担を強化し、 銀行顧客の富裕層や資
産形成層にもニーズに応じて、 証券がサービスを提供す
る動きが寄与している。
　さらに地域銀行において、 銀行の個人向け投資信託事
業を大手証券等との包括提携の下で共同運営する動き
も、 証券比率の上昇に作用している。
　一方で、 銀行等の純資産額も近年は堅調に伸びてお
り、 比率低下は、 銀行の落ち込みによるものではなく、 証
券の伸びが上回った影響が大きい。
　もっ とも、 「銀行と証券のどちらが高成長か」 という比較
より、 顧客ニーズに応じた役割分担の重要性が増してい
る。 2026年7月に政府が策定した 「金融戦略」 も、 インベ
ス トメン トチェーンの機能強化を掲げている。 多様な資産
運用ニーズに各金融サービスの担い手が強みを活かし
て応えることが、 資産運用立国の深化につながろう。
資産運用立国の深化に求められる取組み
資料1 公募投資信託 販売チャネル別純資産額推移
